This runs a converter that bundles the real component code (from Storybook or a bare package) and uploads it. Use when the user runs /design-sync or says \"sync my design system to Claude Design\".","argumentHint":"[<project hint, e.g. \"Acme DS\">]","builtin":true},{"name":"dataviz","description":"Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"update-config","description":"Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"verify","description":"Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"debug","description":"Enable debug logging for this session and help diagnose issues","argumentHint":"[issue description]","builtin":true},{"name":"code-review","description":"Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs (plus reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups where the model's review recipe covers them) at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.","argumentHint":"[low|medium|high|xhigh|max|ultra] [--fix] [--comment] [<pr#>|<branch>|<path>]","aliases":["review"],"builtin":true},{"name":"simplify","description":"Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.","argumentHint":"[<target>]","builtin":true},{"name":"batch","description":"Research and plan a large-scale change, then execute it in parallel across 5–30 isolated worktree agents that each open a PR.","argumentHint":"<instruction>","builtin":true},{"name":"fewer-permission-prompts","description":"Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"doctor","description":"Health-check the user's Claude Code setup and fix issues: diagnose installation health — what the `claude doctor` terminal diagnostics cover — from local data (duplicate or leftover installs, PATH, unparseable settings files, broken or colliding agent definitions, skills whose frontmatter fails to parse); find unused skills, MCP servers, and plugins versus their context cost and disable dead weight; deduplicate local CLAUDE.md files against checked-in ones; trim checked-in CLAUDE.md files by cutting content a session could derive from the codebase (directory layouts, tech-stack lists, architecture overviews) while keeping gotchas, rationale, and non-standard conventions; migrate always-loaded CLAUDE.md guidance into lazy skills and nested CLAUDE.md files; flag slow hooks and context-heavy extensions; check the installed version is current; make auto mode the default permission mode; and pre-approve frequently denied read-only commands. Use when the user asks for a doctor run, checkup, audit, tune-up, or cleanup of their Claude Code setup or configuration.","argumentHint":"","aliases":["checkup"],"builtin":true},{"name":"loop","description":"Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). and mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser tool instead. It covers the pane's persistent sign-ins, tabs and preview_start, reading pages as text, site approvals, what the pane cannot open, and what to do when it cannot be reached. It is not for Claude in Chrome (mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["built-in-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:chrome-browser","description":"Read this skill before the first step that uses Claude in Chrome, the browser extension whose tools are named mcp__claude-in-chrome__* (also called Chrome, the browser extension, or the external browser) and which acts in the person's real Chrome with their own sign-ins; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__claude-in-chrome tool instead. It covers loading the tools in one ToolSearch call, checking the person's open tabs and working in a new tab, site permissions, GIF recordings, console logs, dialogs to avoid, and when to stop and ask. It is not for the built-in browser (mcp__Claude_Browser__* or mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["chrome-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:computer-use","description":"Read this skill before the first step of any request to do something in an app on the person's own computer (Notes, Finder, System Settings, any desktop app), to look at their screen, or for \"computer use\". Computer use (desktop control) lets Claude take screenshots of the person's desktop and control it with clicks, typing and scrolling through the Claude desktop app; its tools are named mcp__computer-use__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__computer_* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before computer use is turned on for a conversation there may be no such tools, only an enable__mcp__remote-devices__computer tool, which turns it on. It covers turning it on, picking the right tool, the access flow, and the safety rules for tiered apps, links and financial actions. It is not for websites, which go through Claude in Chrome or the built-in browser and their own skills. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["computer-use"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:deep-research","description":"Use this skill when the user's prompt requires (1) researching a topic across multiple sources, comparing options or alternatives, analyzing trends or history, understanding markets or industries, or reviewing literature or studies and (2) synthesizing that research into a comprehensive, narrative report. If you're planning to search the web or internal knowledge bases, consider using this skill. This skill coordinates research subagents, so use it only when you have a tool for spawning subagents (the Agent or Task tool); otherwise, research the question directly. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":""},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docs","description":"docs (living docs people share, comment on and edit; use only when the user asks for one: names a doc, document, page, memo, spec, PRD, runbook or write-up, asks for somewhere to share or keep editing something, or says yes to your doc offer; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat (at most a one-line doc offer); a report, status update, recap or \"something I can send them\" with no form named → ask first: reply, doc or file?; tabs hold tables and live charts too; a pasted claude.ai/code/artifact/… link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; not HTML pages, apps or plain chat answers; a .docx/.pptx/.xlsx/PDF asked for by name → that format's skill): asked for one → no docs-connector instructions in context? call the docs connector's `guide` with topic.instructions first, then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached. Documenting code means docstrings or repo docs, not a doc. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docs"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docx","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:import-memory","description":"Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["import-memory"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:morning","description":"Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["morning"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pdf","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pdf"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pptx","description":"Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pptx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:setup-writing-style","description":"Learns how the user writes from their own sent messages and docs, and builds a voice profile so future drafts sound like them instead of generic AI. The profile is saved as the my-writing-style skill. Use when the user asks to set up, learn, or capture their writing voice, or complains that drafts sound generic or unlike them and no my-writing-style profile exists. Only for drafting text the user will send as themselves, not for Claude's own replies. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["setup-writing-style"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:skill-creator","description":"Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["skill-creator"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:xlsx","description":"Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["xlsx"]}],"uuid":"6197c966-11af-4a75-8836-3add92e004fc","session_id":"b73213a0-f835-4f7c-b793-eff657206976"}

{ ForgotPasswordDto, ResetPasswordDto } from './dto/password-reset.dto';\nimport { AuditedOperation } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/audit';\nimport { ThrottlePasswordReset } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/security';\nimport {\n  BadGatewayException,\n  Controller,\n  Post,\n  Body,\n  HttpCode,\n  HttpStatus,\n  Logger,\n  BadRequestException,\n  Inject,\n  Req,\n  SetMetadata,\n} from '@nestjs/common';\nimport { ClientProxy } from '@nestjs/microservices';\nimport { ApiTags } from '@nestjs/swagger';\nimport {\n  AUTH_PUBLIC_COMMAND_SUBJECTS,\n  type PublicRequestPasswordResetCommand,\n  type PublicRequestPasswordResetResult,\n  type PublicResetPasswordCommand,\n  type PublicResetPasswordResult,\n} from '@platform/event-contracts';\nimport { IsEmail, IsString, MinLength, MaxLength } from 'class-validator';\nimport { catchError, firstValueFrom, throwError, timeout } from 'rxjs';\n\nconst DEFAULT_AUTH_NATS_TIMEOUT_MS = 15_000;\n\n// Mark endpoints as public (bypass auth guard)\nconst IS_PUBLIC_KEY = 'isPublic';\nconst Public = () => SetMetadata(IS_PUBLIC_KEY, true);\n\n/** Loose non-empty IP shape for the proxy-header fast path in getIpAddress. */\nconst NONEMPTY_IP = /^[0-9a-fA-F:.]+$/;\n\ntype MinimalRequest = {\n  ip?: string;\n  headers?: Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>;\n};\n\n@ApiTags('Authentication')\n@Controller('auth')\nexport class PasswordResetController {\n  private readonly logger = new Logger(PasswordResetController.name);\n  private readonly timeoutMs: number;\n\n  constructor(\n    @Inject('AUTH_NATS_CLIENT')\n    private readonly authNatsClient: ClientProxy,\n  ) {\n    const configured = parseInt(process.env['AUTH_NATS_TIMEOUT_MS'] ?? '', 10);\n    this.timeoutMs =\n      Number.isFinite(configured) && configured > 0 ? configured : DEFAULT_AUTH_NATS_TIMEOUT_MS;\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Request password reset - sends email with reset link\n   * Always returns success to prevent email enumeration\n   */\n  @AuditedOperation({ resource: 'PasswordReset', action: 'FORGOT_PASSWORD' })\n  @Post('forgot-password')\n  @Public()\n  @ThrottlePasswordReset()\n  @HttpCode(HttpStatus.OK)\n  async forgotPassword(\n    @Body() dto: ForgotPasswordDto,\n    @Req() request: MinimalRequest,\n  ): Promise<{ success: boolean; message: string }> {\n    try {\n      await this.sendAuthCommand<\n        PublicRequestPasswordResetCommand,\n        PublicRequestPasswordResetResult\n      >(AUTH_PUBLIC_COMMAND_SUBJECTS.REQUEST_PASSWORD_RESET, {\n        email: dto.email.toLowerCase(),\n        ipAddress: this.getIpAddress(request),\n        correlationId: this.getCorrelationId(request),\n      });\n    } catch (error) {\n      // Log error but don't reveal it to the user\n      this.logger.error(\n        `Auth-service password reset request failed: ${(error as Error).message}`,\n        (error as Error).stack,\n      );\n    }\n\n    // Always return success to prevent email enumeration\n    return {\n      success: true,\n      message: 'If an account with that email exists, a password reset link has been sent.',\n    };\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Reset password using token from email\n   */\n  @AuditedOperation({ resource: 'Password', action: 'RESET' })\n  @Post('reset-password')\n  @Public()\n  @ThrottlePasswordReset()\n  @HttpCode(HttpStatus.OK)\n  async resetPassword(\n    @Body() dto: ResetPasswordDto,\n    @Req() request: MinimalRequest,\n  ): Promise<{ success: boolean; message: string }> {\n    const result = await this.sendAuthCommand<\n      PublicResetPasswordCommand,\n      PublicResetPasswordResult\n    >(AUTH_PUBLIC_COMMAND_SUBJECTS.RESET_PASSWORD, {\n      token: dto.token,\n      newPassword: dto.newPassword,\n      ipAddress: this.getIpAddress(request),\n      userAgent: this.getHeader(request, 'user-agent'),\n      correlationId: this.getCorrelationId(request),\n    });\n\n    if (!result.success && result.errorCode === 'INVALID_OR_EXPIRED_TOKEN') {\n      throw new BadRequestException('Invalid or expired reset token');\n    }\n    if (!result.success) {\n      throw new BadGatewayException(result.error ?? 'Auth-service password reset failed');\n    }\n\n    return {\n      success: true,\n      message: 'Password has been reset successfully.',\n    };\n  }\n\n  private async sendAuthCommand<TCommand, TResult>(\n    subject: string,\n    command: TCommand,\n  ): Promise<TResult> {\n    try {\n      return await firstValueFrom(\n        this.authNatsClient.send<TResult, TCommand>(subject, command).pipe(\n          timeout(this.timeoutMs),\n          catchError((err: Error) => {\n            this.logger.error(`NATS request failed: subject=${subject}, error=${err.message}`);\n            return throwError(() => err);\n          }),\n        ),\n      );\n    } catch (err: unknown) {\n      const message = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err);\n      throw new BadGatewayException(`Auth service error: ${message}`);\n    }\n  }\n\n  private getIpAddress(request: MinimalRequest): string | undefined {\n    // SEC-LOW-085 (2026-08-23 scan №32): the audit IP must resolve the same\n    // way everywhere — proxy-set X-Real-IP (nginx) first, then Express\n    // req.ip (TRUST_PROXY), and the spoofable client-prepended XFF value\n    // only as a last resort (nginx appends the real client after it).\n    const realIp = this.getHeader(request, 'x-real-ip');\n    if (realIp && NONEMPTY_IP.test(realIp)) {\n      return realIp;\n    }\n    if (request.ip) {\n      return request.ip;\n    }\n    return this.getHeader(request, 'x-forwarded-for')?.split(',')[0]?.trim();\n  }\n\n  private getCorrelationId(request: MinimalRequest): string | undefined {\n    return this.getHeader(request, 'x-correlation-id') ?? this.getHeader(request, 'x-request-id');\n  }\n\n  private getHeader(request: MinimalRequest, name: string): string | undefined {\n    const value = request.headers?.[name] ?? request.headers?.[name.toLowerCase()];\n    return Array.isArray(value) ? value[0] : value;\n  }\n}\n","numLines":175,"startLine":1,"totalLines":175}}}

ts-node\nimport { relative } from 'node:path';\n\nimport ts from 'typescript';\n\nimport {\n  collectFiles,\n  filterFilesBySnapshot,\n  normalizeWorkspacePath,\n  readWorkspaceFile,\n  requireScanRoots,\n  resolveInsideWorkspace as resolveAdapterPath,\n  workspacePathExists,\n} from './adapter-fs';\n\ntype FindingRule =\n  | 'public_write_endpoint_without_allowlist'\n  | 'mutation_missing_role_boundary'\n  | 'controller_missing_guard_boundary'\n  | 'dangerous_html_without_sanitizer'\n  | 'raw_security_sensitive_import';\n\ninterface AdapterInput {\n  readonly roots?: readonly string[];\n  readonly allowlist?: readonly string[];\n  readonly repo_snapshot?: { readonly allowed_paths?: readonly string[]; readonly snapshot_hash?: string; readonly repo_state_id?: string };\n}\n\ninterface EvidenceRef {\n  readonly path: string;\n  readonly line?: number;\n}\n\ninterface AdapterObservation {\n  readonly id: string;\n  readonly type: string;\n  readonly path?: string;\n  readonly line?: number;\n  readonly name?: string;\n  readonly details?: Record<string, unknown>;\n}\n\ninterface AdapterFinding {\n  readonly id: string;\n  readonly rule: FindingRule;\n  readonly severity: 'medium' | 'high';\n  readonly path: string;\n  readonly line?: number;\n  readonly message: string;\n  readonly evidence: readonly EvidenceRef[];\n  readonly details?: Record<string, unknown>;\n}\n\ninterface MemoryCandidate {\n  readonly belief_id: string;\n  readonly claim: string;\n  readonly confidence: number;\n  readonly evidence_refs: readonly string[];\n  readonly source_tool_id: string;\n}\n\ninterface AriaOutput {\n  readonly observations: readonly AdapterObservation[];\n  readonly findings: readonly AdapterFinding[];\n  readonly read_paths: readonly string[];\n  readonly evidence_sources: readonly string[];\n  readonly belief_candidates: readonly MemoryCandidate[];\n  readonly cost_units: number;\n  readonly metadata: Record<string, unknown>;\n}\n\ninterface SourceUnit {\n  readonly path: string;\n  readonly relativePath: string;\n  readonly text: string;\n  readonly sourceFile: ts.SourceFile;\n}\n\ninterface AnalysisResult {\n  readonly observations: AdapterObservation[];\n  readonly findings: AdapterFinding[];\n  readonly readPaths: string[];\n}\n\ninterface SecurityContext {\n  readonly serviceGuards: ReadonlyMap<string, readonly string[]>;\n}\n\nconst ROUTE_DECORATORS = new Set(['Get', 'Post', 'Put', 'Patch', 'Delete', 'Query', 'Mutation']);\nconst WRITE_DECORATORS = new Set(['Post', 'Put', 'Patch', 'Delete', 'Mutation']);\nconst SECURITY_DECORATORS = new Set(['UseGuards', 'Roles', 'Permissions', 'Public', 'SkipTenantGuard']);\nconst SAFE_RAW_IMPORT_ALLOWLIST = [\n  'apps/ai-service/src/agent/agent-runner.service.ts',\n  'tools/eslint-rules/no-claude-sdk-raw-call.ts',\n];\n\nexport function analyzeSecurityBoundaries(input: AdapterInput, workspaceRoot = process.cwd()): AriaOutput {\n  const roots = requireScanRoots('security-boundary-adapter', input.roots);\n  const allowlist = new Set((input.allowlist ?? []).map(normalizePath));\n  const files = roots\n    .map((root) => resolveInsideWorkspace(workspaceRoot, root))\n    .filter((root) => workspacePathExists(root))\n    .flatMap((root) => collectSourceFiles(root));\n  const snapshotFiles = filterFilesBySnapshot(files, workspaceRoot, input);\n  const units = snapshotFiles.map((file) => readSourceUnit(file, workspaceRoot));\n  const result: AnalysisResult = {\n    observations: [],\n    findings: [],\n    readPaths: units.map((unit) => unit.relativePath),\n  };\n  const context: SecurityContext = {\n    serviceGuards: collectServiceGuards(units),\n  };\n\n  for (const unit of units) {\n    analyzeSecurityUnit(unit, result, allowlist, context);\n  }\n\n  result.observations.sort(compareById);\n  result.findings.sort(compareById);\n  result.readPaths.sort();\n  const evidenceSources = Array.from(\n    new Set(result.findings.flatMap((finding) => finding.evidence.map((evidence) => evidence.path))),\n  ).sort();\n\n  return {\n    observations: result.observations,\n    findings: result.findings,\n    read_paths: Array.from(new Set(result.readPaths)).sort(),\n    evidence_sources: evidenceSources,\n    belief_candidates: [\n      {\n        belief_id: 'security-boundary:route-and-sensitive-sink-surface',\n        claim: 'backend and web route boundaries expose guard/decorator and sensitive sink surfaces that need deterministic security checks',\n        confidence: 0.8,\n        evidence_refs: ['apps/**/*.ts', 'web/**/*.tsx', 'libs/backend-common/src/**/*.ts'],\n        source_tool_id: 'security-boundary-adapter',\n      },\n    ],\n    cost_units: Array.from(new Set(result.readPaths)).length,\n    metadata: {\n      adapter: 'security-boundary-adapter',\n      roots: roots.map(String).sort(),\n      files_scanned: snapshotFiles.length,\n      findings_count: result.findings.length,\n      allowlist_count: allowlist.size,\n    },\n  };\n}\n\nfunction analyzeSecurityUnit(\n  unit: SourceUnit,\n  result: AnalysisResult,\n  allowlist: ReadonlySet<string>,\n  context: SecurityContext,\n): void {\n  const allowlisted = allowlist.has(unit.relativePath);\n  analyzeEndpoints(unit, result, allowlisted, context);\n  analyzeDangerousHtml(unit, result, allowlisted);\n  analyzeRawSecurityImports(unit, result, allowlisted);\n  analyzeExistingGates(unit, result);\n}\n\nfunction analyzeEndpoints(unit: SourceUnit, result: AnalysisResult, allowlisted: boolean, context: SecurityContext): void {\n  const inheritedGuards = serviceGuardsForUnit(unit, context);\n  visit(unit.sourceFile, (node) => {\n    if (!ts.isMethodDeclaration(node) && !ts.isClassDeclaration(node)) {\n      return;\n    }\n    const decorators = decoratorNames(node);\n    const routeDecorators = decorators.filter((name) => ROUTE_DECORATORS.has(name));\n    if (routeDecorators.length === 0) {\n      return;\n    }\n    const line = lineOf(unit.sourceFile, node);\n    const name = ts.isMethodDeclaration(node) ? node.name.getText(unit.sourceFile) : node.name?.text ?? '<anonymous>';\n    const classNode = ts.isMethodDeclaration(node) ? nearestClass(node) : node;\n    const classDecorators = classNode ? decoratorNames(classNode) : [];\n    const allDecorators = new Set([...decorators, ...classDecorators]);\n    const hasLocalGuardBoundary = allDecorators.has('UseGuards') || allDecorators.has('Roles') || allDecorators.has('Permissions');\n    const hasInheritedGuardBoundary = inheritedGuards.length > 0;\n    const hasGuardBoundary = hasLocalGuardBoundary || hasInheritedGuardBoundary;\n    const isPublic = allDecorators.has('Public');\n    const isSkippedTenant = allDecorators.has('SkipTenantGuard');\n    const writes = routeDecorators.some((decorator) => WRITE_DECORATORS.has(decorator));\n\n    result.observations.push({\n      id: `security-boundary-endpoint:${unit.relativePath}:${line}:${name}`,\n      type: 'security_boundary_endpoint',\n      path: unit.relativePath,\n      line,\n      name,\n      details: {\n        routeDecorators: routeDecorators.sort(),\n        securityDecorators: [...allDecorators].filter((decorator) => SECURITY_DECORATORS.has(decorator)).sort(),\n        inheritedGuards,\n        hasLocalGuardBoundary,\n        hasInheritedGuardBoundary,\n        hasGuardBoundary,\n        isPublic,\n        isSkippedTenant,\n        writes,\n        allowlisted,\n      },\n    });\n\n    if (allowlisted) {\n      return;\n    }\n    if (isPublic && writes && !isSkippedTenant) {\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `public-write-endpoint-without-allowlist:${unit.relativePath}:${line}`,\n        rule: 'public_write_endpoint_without_allowlist',\n        severity: 'high',\n        path: unit.relativePath,\n        line,\n        message: 'Public write endpoint requires an explicit allowlist or tenant-skip rationale.',\n        evidence: [{ path: unit.relativePath, line }],\n        details: { routeDecorators: routeDecorators.sort(), name },\n      });\n    }\n    if (routeDecorators.includes('Mutation') && !hasGuardBoundary && !isPublic) {\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `mutation-missing-role-boundary:${unit.relativePath}:${line}`,\n        rule: 'mutation_missing_role_boundary',\n        severity: 'medium',\n        path: unit.relativePath,\n        line,\n        message: 'GraphQL mutation has no method/class role, permission, or guard boundary.',\n        evidence: [{ path: unit.relativePath, line }],\n        details: { name },\n      });\n    }\n    if (!isPublic && !hasGuardBoundary && routeDecorators.some((decorator) => decorator !== 'Query')) {\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `controller-missing-guard-boundary:${unit.relativePath}:${line}`,\n        rule: 'controller_missing_guard_boundary',\n        severity: 'medium',\n        path: unit.relativePath,\n        line,\n        message: 'Route boundary has no explicit public marker or method/class guard boundary.',\n        evidence: [{ path: unit.relativePath, line }],\n        details: { routeDecorators: routeDecorators.sort(), name },\n      });\n    }\n  });\n}\n\nfunction collectServiceGuards(units: readonly SourceUnit[]): ReadonlyMap<string, readonly string[]> {\n  const contexts = new Map<string, readonly string[]>();\n  for (const unit of units) {\n    if (!unit.relativePath.endsWith('/app.module.ts')) {\n      continue;\n    }\n    const guards = new Set<string>();\n    visit(unit.sourceFile, (node) => {\n      if (!ts.isObjectLiteralExpression(node) || !objectHasPropertyText(node, 'provide', 'APP_GUARD')) {\n        return;\n      }\n      for (const property of node.properties) {\n        if (!ts.isPropertyAssignment(property)) {\n          continue;\n        }\n        const name = property.name.getText(unit.sourceFile);\n        if (name !== 'useClass' && name !== 'useFactory' && name !== 'useExisting') {\n          continue;\n        }\n        for (const guard of guardNamesInText(property.initializer.getText(unit.sourceFile))) {\n          guards.add(guard);\n        }\n      }\n    });\n    const serviceRoot = unit.relativePath.replace(/\\/app\\.module\\.ts$/, '');\n    contexts.set(serviceRoot, Array.from(guards).sort());\n  }\n  return contexts;\n}\n\nfunction objectHasPropertyText(node: ts.ObjectLiteralExpression, propertyName: string, expectedText: string): boolean {\n  return node.properties.some((property) => {\n    if (!ts.isPropertyAssignment(property) || property.name.getText() !== propertyName) {\n      return false;\n    }\n    return property.initializer.getText().includes(expectedText);\n  });\n}\n\nfunction guardNamesInText(text: string): string[] {\n  const matches = text.match(/\\b[A-Za-z0-9_]*(?:Guard|AuthGuard)\\b/g) ?? [];\n  return [...new Set(matches)].filter((name) =>\n    /Guard$/.test(name)\n    || ['AuthGuard', 'GqlAuthGuard', 'JwtAuthGuard', 'PlatformAdminGuard', 'ServiceIdentityGuard'].includes(name),\n  );\n}\n\nfunction serviceGuardsForUnit(unit: SourceUnit, context: SecurityContext): readonly string[] {\n  const candidates = [...context.serviceGuards.entries()]\n    .filter(([root]) => unit.relativePath.startsWith(`${root}/`))\n    .sort((a, b) => b[0].length - a[0].length);\n  return candidates[0]?.[1] ?? [];\n}\n\nfunction analyzeDangerousHtml(unit: SourceUnit, result: AnalysisResult, allowlisted: boolean): void {\n  if (!unit.relativePath.endsWith('.tsx')) {\n    return;\n  }\n\n  const fileHasSanitizerCall = hasRuntimeSanitizerCall(unit.sourceFile);\n  for (const sink of dangerousHtmlSinks(unit)) {\n    const hasSanitizer = sink.hasLocalSanitizer || fileHasSanitizerCall;\n    result.observations.push({\n      id: `security-sensitive-sink:${unit.relativePath}:${sink.line}:dangerouslySetInnerHTML`,\n      type: 'security_sensitive_sink',\n      path: unit.relativePath,\n      line: sink.line,\n      name: 'dangerouslySetInnerHTML',\n      details: { hasSanitizer, hasLocalSanitizer: sink.hasLocalSanitizer, fileHasSanitizerCall, allowlisted },\n    });\n    if (!allowlisted && !hasSanitizer) {\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `dangerous-html-without-sanitizer:${unit.relativePath}:${sink.line}`,\n        rule: 'dangerous_html_without_sanitizer',\n        severity: 'high',\n        path: unit.relativePath,\n        line: sink.line,\n        message: 'dangerouslySetInnerHTML is used without an obvious sanitizer in the sink expression or same file.',\n        evidence: [{ path: unit.relativePath, line: sink.line }],\n      });\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nfunction dangerousHtmlSinks(unit: SourceUnit): Array<{ readonly line: number; readonly hasLocalSanitizer: boolean }> {\n  const sinks: Array<{ readonly line: number; readonly hasLocalSanitizer: boolean }> = [];\n  visit(unit.sourceFile, (node) => {\n    // `JsxAttributeName` is Identifier | JsxNamespacedName since TS 5.1;\n    // only the identifier form can be this sink, and `.text` exists only\n    // on it — the narrowing is the type fix, not a behavioural change.\n    if (\n      ts.isJsxAttribute(node) &&\n      ts.isIdentifier(node.name) &&\n      node.name.text === 'dangerouslySetInnerHTML'\n    ) {\n      sinks.push({\n        line: lineOf(unit.sourceFile, node),\n        hasLocalSanitizer: node.initializer ? hasRuntimeSanitizerCall(node.initializer) : false,\n      });\n      return;\n    }\n    if (ts.isPropertyAssignment(node) && propertyNameText(node.name, unit.sourceFile) === 'dangerouslySetInnerHTML') {\n      sinks.push({\n        line: lineOf(unit.sourceFile, node),\n        hasLocalSanitizer: hasRuntimeSanitizerCall(node.initializer),\n      });\n    }\n  });\n  return sinks;\n}\n\nfunction hasRuntimeSanitizerCall(node: ts.Node): boolean {\n  let found = false;\n  visit(node, (child) => {\n    if (found || !ts.isCallExpression(child)) {\n      return;\n    }\n    const expression = child.expression;\n    if (ts.isIdentifier(expression) && ['sanitizeHtml', 'sanitize'].includes(expression.text)) {\n      found = true;\n      return;\n    }\n    if (\n      ts.isPropertyAccessExpression(expression)\n      && expression.name.text === 'sanitize'\n      && expression.expression.getText().endsWith('DOMPurify')\n    ) {\n      found = true;\n    }\n  });\n  return found;\n}\n\nfunction propertyNameText(name: ts.PropertyName, sourceFile: ts.SourceFile): string | undefined {\n  if (ts.isIdentifier(name) || ts.isStringLiteral(name) || ts.isNumericLiteral(name)) {\n    return name.text;\n  }\n  return name.getText(sourceFile);\n}\n\nfunction analyzeRawSecurityImports(unit: SourceUnit, result: AnalysisResult, allowlisted: boolean): void {\n  visit(unit.sourceFile, (node) => {\n    if (!ts.isImportDeclaration(node) || !ts.isStringLiteral(node.moduleSpecifier)) {\n      return;\n    }\n    const moduleName = node.moduleSpecifier.text;\n    if (moduleName !== '@anthropic-ai/sdk') {\n      return;\n    }\n    const line = lineOf(unit.sourceFile, node);\n    const builtInAllowlisted = SAFE_RAW_IMPORT_ALLOWLIST.includes(unit.relativePath);\n    result.observations.push({\n      id: `security-sensitive-import:${unit.relativePath}:${line}:${moduleName}`,\n      type: 'security_sensitive_sink',\n      path: unit.relativePath,\n      line,\n      name: moduleName,\n      details: { allowlisted: allowlisted || builtInAllowlisted },\n    });\n    if (!allowlisted && !builtInAllowlisted) {\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `raw-security-sensitive-import:${unit.relativePath}:${line}`,\n        rule: 'raw_security_sensitive_import',\n        severity: 'high',\n        path: unit.relativePath,\n        line,\n        message: 'Raw security-sensitive SDK import should go through the approved wrapper or lint-enforced allowlist.',\n        evidence: [{ path: unit.relativePath, line }],\n        details: { moduleName },\n      });\n    }\n  });\n}\n\nfunction analyzeExistingGates(unit: SourceUnit, result: AnalysisResult): void {\n  if (!unit.relativePath.includes('tools/eslint-rules/')) {\n    return;\n  }\n  if (/no-(direct-event-publish|claude-sdk-raw-call|bare-graphql-query-string)/.test(unit.relativePath)) {\n    result.observations.push({\n      id: `security-existing-gate:${unit.relativePath}`,\n      type: 'security_existing_gate',\n      path: unit.relativePath,\n      line: 1,\n      name: unit.relativePath.split('/').pop(),\n    });\n  }\n}\n\nfunction decoratorNames(node: ts.Node): string[] {\n  const decorators = ts.canHaveDecorators(node) ? ts.getDecorators(node) ?? [] : [];\n  return decorators\n    .map((decorator) => decorator.expression)\n    .map((expression) => (ts.isCallExpression(expression) ? expression.expression : expression))\n    .map((expression) => expression.getText())\n    .map((text) => text.split('.').pop() ?? text)\n    .sort();\n}\n\nfunction nearestClass(node: ts.Node): ts.ClassDeclaration | undefined {\n  let current: ts.Node | undefined = node.parent;\n  while (current) {\n    if (ts.isClassDeclaration(current)) {\n      return current;\n    }\n    current = current.parent;\n  }\n  return undefined;\n}\n\nfunction readSourceUnit(path: string, workspaceRoot: string): SourceUnit {\n  const text = readWorkspaceFile(path);\n  return {\n    path,\n    relativePath: normalizePath(relative(workspaceRoot, path)),\n    text,\n    sourceFile: ts.createSourceFile(path, text, ts.ScriptTarget.Latest, true),\n  };\n}\n\nfunction collectSourceFiles(root: string): readonly string[] {\n  return collectFiles(root, {\n    extensions: ['.ts', '.tsx'],\n    includeExcludedDir: (name) => name === '__tests__',\n    includeFile: (name) => !isTestFile(name),\n  });\n}\n\nfunction isTestFile(name: string): boolean {\n  return name.endsWith('.spec.ts') || name.endsWith('.test.ts') || name.endsWith('.d.ts');\n}\n\nfunction resolveInsideWorkspace(workspaceRoot: string, requestedPath: string): string {\n  return resolveAdapterPath(workspaceRoot, requestedPath);\n}\n\nfunction lineOf(sourceFile: ts.SourceFile, node: ts.Node): number {\n  return sourceFile.getLineAndCharacterOfPosition(node.getStart(sourceFile)).line + 1;\n}\n\nfunction visit(node: ts.Node, visitor: (node: ts.Node) => void): void {\n  visitor(node);\n  ts.forEachChild(node, (child) => visit(child, visitor));\n}\n\nfunction compareById(a: { readonly id: string }, b: { readonly id: string }): number {\n  return a.id.localeCompare(b.id);\n}\n\nfunction normalizePath(path: string): string {\n  return normalizeWorkspacePath(path);\n}\n\nfunction readStdin(): Promise<string> {\n  return new Promise((resolvePromise, reject) => {\n    let input = '';\n    process.stdin.setEncoding('utf8');\n    // setEncoding('utf8') makes every chunk a string at runtime, but the\n    // stream's declared chunk type stays `string | Buffer` — concatenating the\n    // union is what the type checker rejects. Narrow at the boundary rather\n    // than widening `input` (kernel-dead-wire-adapter is the converged shape).\n    process.stdin.on('data', (chunk: string | Buffer) => {\n      input += typeof chunk === 'string' ? chunk : chunk.toString('utf8');\n    });\n    process.stdin.on('end', () => resolvePromise(input));\n    process.stdin.on('error', reject);\n  });\n}\n\nasync function main(): Promise<void> {\n  const rawInput = await readStdin();\n  const input = rawInput.trim().length > 0 ? (JSON.parse(rawInput) as AdapterInput) : {};\n  process.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify(analyzeSecurityBoundaries(input))}\\n`);\n}\n\nif (require.main === module) {\n  main().catch((error: unknown) => {\n    const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);\n    process.stderr.write(`${message}\\n`);\n    process.exit(1);\n  });\n}\n","numLines":531,"startLine":1,"totalLines":531}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TfxLEgUKEms6jEcN8T7HCQ","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport { createParamDecorator, ExecutionContext, BadRequestException } from '@nestjs/common';\n2\timport { GqlExecutionContext } from '@nestjs/graphql';\n3\timport { TenantRequest } from '../types/tenant-request.interface';\n4\t\n5\t/**\n6\t * Tenant Context Decorator — extracts the authenticated tenant ID from the request.\n7\t *\n8\t * SECURITY (C-03): This decorator ONLY reads from two trusted sources:\n9\t *   1. `req.user.tenantId` — set by JwtAuthGuard from the verified JWT claim.\n10\t *   2. `req.tenantId` — set by TenantGuard after validation.\n11\t *\n12\t * Headers (X-Tenant-Id), query parameters, and request body are NEVER consulted.\n13\t * Those sources are attacker-controlled and were previously exploitable when\n14\t * combined with @SkipTenantGuard() to inject arbitrary tenant IDs.\n15\t */\n16\texport const Tenant = createParamDecorator(\n17\t  (data: unknown, ctx: ExecutionContext): string => {\n18\t    const contextType = ctx.getType<string>();\n19\t\n20\t    if (contextType === 'graphql') {\n21\t      const gqlCtx = GqlExecutionContext.create(ctx);\n22\t      const request = gqlCtx.getContext().req as TenantRequest;\n23\t      return extractTenantId(request);\n24\t    }\n25\t\n26\t    const request = ctx.switchToHttp().getRequest<TenantRequest>();\n27\t    return extractTenantId(request);\n28\t  },\n29\t);\n30\t\n31\t/**\n32\t * Extract tenant ID exclusively from trusted, server-set sources.\n33\t *\n34\t * SECURITY: Only two sources are trusted:\n35\t *   - `req.user.tenantId`: Decoded from a cryptographically verified JWT by JwtAuthGuard.\n36\t *   - `req.tenantId`: Explicitly set by TenantGuard after validating the user's\n37\t *     JWT tenantId claim (or SUPER_ADMIN's X-Act-As-Tenant header).\n38\t *\n39\t * All other sources (headers, query params, body) are untrusted and intentionally\n40\t * excluded to prevent tenant spoofing attacks.\n41\t */\n42\tfunction extractTenantId(request: TenantRequest): string {\n43\t  // 1. Prefer JWT claim — cryptographically verified by JwtAuthGuard\n44\t  if (request.user?.tenantId) {\n45\t    return request.user.tenantId;\n46\t  }\n47\t\n48\t  // 2. Fall back to TenantGuard-validated value\n49\t  if (request.tenantId) {\n50\t    return request.tenantId;\n51\t  }\n52\t\n53\t  throw new BadRequestException(\n54\t    'Tenant ID not found in request context. Ensure the request is authenticated and the tenant guard has executed.',\n55\t  );\n56\t}\n57\t\n58\t/**\n59\t * Optional Tenant Decorator — returns the authenticated tenant ID or undefined.\n60\t *\n61\t * SECURITY (C-03): Same trusted-source-only policy as @Tenant(). Never reads\n62\t * from headers, query params, or request body. Returns undefined instead of\n63\t * throwing when no tenant context is available.\n64\t */\n65\texport const OptionalTenant = createParamDecorator(\n66\t  (data: unknown, ctx: ExecutionContext): string | undefined => {\n67\t    try {\n68\t      const contextType = ctx.getType<string>();\n69\t\n70\t      if (contextType === 'graphql') {\n71\t        const gqlCtx = GqlExecutionContext.create(ctx);\n72\t        const request = gqlCtx.getContext().req as TenantRequest;\n73\t        return extractTenantIdSafe(request);\n74\t      }\n75\t\n76\t      const request = ctx.switchToHttp().getRequest<TenantRequest>();\n77\t      return extractTenantIdSafe(request);\n78\t    } catch {\n79\t      return undefined;\n80\t    }\n81\t  },\n82\t);\n83\t\n84\t/**\n85\t * Safe tenant ID extraction from trusted sources only.\n86\t * Returns undefined when no tenant context is available.\n87\t */\n88\tfunction extractTenantIdSafe(request: TenantRequest): string | undefined {\n89\t  return request.user?.tenantId || request.tenantId || undefined;\n90\t}\n91\t\n92\t/**\n93\t * @deprecated Use `Tenant` instead.\n94\t * `CurrentTenant` is an alias kept only for backward compatibility with existing\n95\t * callers. Migrate usages to `Tenant` and this alias will be removed in a future\n96\t * major release.\n97\t */\n98\texport { Tenant as CurrentTenant };\n99\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b73213a0-f835-4f7c-b793-eff657206976","uuid":"8ee99505-9af3-4f1d-9620-210bc64bc7b2","timestamp":"2026-09-19T13:44:53.838Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-725e0f69acd6/libs/backend-common/src/decorators/tenant.decorator.ts","content":"import { createParamDecorator, ExecutionContext, BadRequestException } from '@nestjs/common';\nimport { GqlExecutionContext } from '@nestjs/graphql';\nimport { TenantRequest } from '../types/tenant-request.interface';\n\n/**\n * Tenant Context Decorator — extracts the authenticated tenant ID from the request.\n *\n * SECURITY (C-03): This decorator ONLY reads from two trusted sources:\n *   1. `req.user.tenantId` — set by JwtAuthGuard from the verified JWT claim.\n *   2. `req.tenantId` — set by TenantGuard after validation.\n *\n * Headers (X-Tenant-Id), query parameters, and request body are NEVER consulted.\n * Those sources are attacker-controlled and were previously exploitable when\n * combined with @SkipTenantGuard() to inject arbitrary tenant IDs.\n */\nexport const Tenant = createParamDecorator(\n  (data: unknown, ctx: ExecutionContext): string => {\n    const contextType = ctx.getType<string>();\n\n    if (contextType === 'graphql') {\n      const gqlCtx = GqlExecutionContext.create(ctx);\n      const request = gqlCtx.getContext().req as TenantRequest;\n      return extractTenantId(request);\n    }\n\n    const request = ctx.switchToHttp().getRequest<TenantRequest>();\n    return extractTenantId(request);\n  },\n);\n\n/**\n * Extract tenant ID exclusively from trusted, server-set sources.\n *\n * SECURITY: Only two sources are trusted:\n *   - `req.user.tenantId`: Decoded from a cryptographically verified JWT by JwtAuthGuard.\n *   - `req.tenantId`: Explicitly set by TenantGuard after validating the user's\n *     JWT tenantId claim (or SUPER_ADMIN's X-Act-As-Tenant header).\n *\n * All other sources (headers, query params, body) are untrusted and intentionally\n * excluded to prevent tenant spoofing attacks.\n */\nfunction extractTenantId(request: TenantRequest): string {\n  // 1. Prefer JWT claim — cryptographically verified by JwtAuthGuard\n  if (request.user?.tenantId) {\n    return request.user.tenantId;\n  }\n\n  // 2. Fall back to TenantGuard-validated value\n  if (request.tenantId) {\n    return request.tenantId;\n  }\n\n  throw new BadRequestException(\n    'Tenant ID not found in request context. Ensure the request is authenticated and the tenant guard has executed.',\n  );\n}\n\n/**\n * Optional Tenant Decorator — returns the authenticated tenant ID or undefined.\n *\n * SECURITY (C-03): Same trusted-source-only policy as @Tenant(). Never reads\n * from headers, query params, or request body. Returns undefined instead of\n * throwing when no tenant context is available.\n */\nexport const OptionalTenant = createParamDecorator(\n  (data: unknown, ctx: ExecutionContext): string | undefined => {\n    try {\n      const contextType = ctx.getType<string>();\n\n      if (contextType === 'graphql') {\n        const gqlCtx = GqlExecutionContext.create(ctx);\n        const request = gqlCtx.getContext().req as TenantRequest;\n        return extractTenantIdSafe(request);\n      }\n\n      const request = ctx.switchToHttp().getRequest<TenantRequest>();\n      return extractTenantIdSafe(request);\n    } catch {\n      return undefined;\n    }\n  },\n);\n\n/**\n * Safe tenant ID extraction from trusted sources only.\n * Returns undefined when no tenant context is available.\n */\nfunction extractTenantIdSafe(request: TenantRequest): string | undefined {\n  return request.user?.tenantId || request.tenantId || undefined;\n}\n\n/**\n * @deprecated Use `Tenant` instead.\n * `CurrentTenant` is an alias kept only for backward compatibility with existing\n * callers. Migrate usages to `Tenant` and this alias will be removed in a future\n * major release.\n */\nexport { Tenant as CurrentTenant };\n","numLines":99,"startLine":1,"totalLines":99}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_013dDYK2oh6Ei9Nc7eVubrgH","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport {\n2\t  Injectable,\n3\t  CanActivate,\n4\t  ExecutionContext,\n5\t  BadRequestException,\n6\t  ForbiddenException,\n7\t  ServiceUnavailableException,\n8\t  Inject,\n9\t  Logger,\n10\t  Optional,\n11\t} from '@nestjs/common';\n12\timport { GqlExecutionContext } from '@nestjs/graphql';\n13\timport { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';\n14\timport { Reflector } from '@nestjs/core';\n15\timport { SKIP_TENANT_GUARD_KEY, IS_PUBLIC_KEY, Role } from '../decorators/roles.decorator';\n16\timport { TenantRequest } from '../types/tenant-request.interface';\n17\t// IMPORTANT: import from tokens, NOT from `audit-log.service` / `audit-log.entity`.\n18\t// Importing the service would chain through `audit-log.entity` and fire the\n19\t// `@Entity()` decorator on every backend-common consumer, polluting TypeORM's\n20\t// global metadata storage and surfacing as cross-service drift on services\n21\t// that never opted into audit logging (DEFECT-1, INFRA-CRITICAL-021).\n22\timport {\n23\t  AUDIT_LOG_SERVICE,\n24\t  AuditSeverity,\n25\t  type IAuditLogService,\n26\t} from '../audit/audit-log.tokens';\n27\t\n28\t/** UUID v4 format validator. */\n29\tconst UUID_REGEX = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i;\n30\t\n31\t/**\n32\t * Tenant Guard — enforces tenant isolation for every authenticated request.\n33\t *\n34\t * SECURITY (C-04): Tenant ID sources have been reduced to the minimum trusted set:\n35\t *\n36\t *   **Regular users** — The ONLY accepted source is `req.user.tenantId`, decoded\n37\t *   from a cryptographically verified JWT by JwtAuthGuard. Query parameters,\n38\t *   request body, and ALL headers (including X-Tenant-Id) are intentionally\n39\t *   excluded. An authenticated user can trivially set any of those values, so\n40\t *   accepting them would allow tenant context spoofing.\n41\t *\n42\t *   **SUPER_ADMIN** — May impersonate a specific tenant only through the\n43\t *   gateway-resolved, HMAC-bound effective-tenant context. Regular subgraphs\n44\t *   consume the verified user assertion; auth-service consumes the verified\n45\t *   gateway service identity because it verifies the user's JWT itself.\n46\t *\n47\t * Skip behaviour:\n48\t * - `@SkipTenantGuard()` skips tenant validation for a single route that\n49\t *   still requires authentication.\n50\t * - `@Public()` marks the endpoint as publicly accessible; TenantGuard\n51\t *   checks both `skipTenantGuard` and `isPublic` metadata so either\n52\t *   decorator is sufficient to bypass tenant validation.\n53\t *\n54\t * SECURITY (H-13): SUPER_ADMIN cross-tenant access is **persistently** audit-logged\n55\t * via AuditLogService with action `SUPER_ADMIN_CROSS_TENANT_ACCESS`. The audit record\n56\t * includes userId, sourceTenantId, targetTenantId, endpoint, timestamp, client IP,\n57\t * and user agent. An ephemeral logger.warn() is also emitted for real-time observability.\n58\t *\n59\t * MFA Step-Up: SUPER_ADMIN cross-tenant access requires `mfaVerified: true` in the JWT\n60\t * claims by default (`MFA_REQUIRED_FOR_CROSS_TENANT` defaults to `'true'`).\n61\t * Set `MFA_REQUIRED_FOR_CROSS_TENANT=false` to opt out during MFA rollout.\n62\t */\n63\t@Injectable()\n64\texport class TenantGuard implements CanActivate {\n65\t  private readonly logger = new Logger(TenantGuard.name);\n66\t  private readonly isProduction = process.env['NODE_ENV'] === 'production';\n67\t\n68\t  /** Whether MFA step-up is required for SUPER_ADMIN cross-tenant access. */\n69\t  private readonly mfaRequiredForCrossTenant: boolean;\n70\t\n71\t  // WHY: Explicit @Inject() — design:paramtypes may not survive all build/runtime environments\n72\t  // (Alpine musl, prod-only deps). Belt-and-suspenders for NestJS DI resolution.\n73\t  // The audit dependency uses the AUDIT_LOG_SERVICE token + IAuditLogService\n74\t  // interface (NOT the concrete class) so this guard never imports the\n75\t  // `AuditLogEntity` decorator, even transitively. Services that wire\n76\t  // `AuditLogModule.forRoot()` provide the token. Production cross-tenant\n77\t  // requests fail closed if that mandatory append capability is unavailable.\n78\t  constructor(\n79\t    @Inject(Reflector) private reflector: Reflector,\n80\t    @Optional() @Inject(AUDIT_LOG_SERVICE) private readonly auditLogService?: IAuditLogService,\n81\t    @Optional() @Inject(ConfigService) private readonly configService?: ConfigService,\n82\t  ) {\n83\t    this.mfaRequiredForCrossTenant =\n84\t      this.configService?.get<string>('MFA_REQUIRED_FOR_CROSS_TENANT', 'true') !== 'false';\n85\t\n86\t    if (!this.auditLogService) {\n87\t      this.logger.warn(\n88\t        JSON.stringify({\n89\t          event: 'cross_tenant_audit_capability_unavailable',\n90\t          productionFailClosed: this.isProduction,\n91\t        }),\n92\t      );\n93\t    }\n94\t  }\n95\t\n96\t  /**\n97\t   * Evaluate tenant isolation rules for the current request.\n98\t   *\n99\t   * SECURITY (ONEMLI-05): Signature is `async` because cross-tenant audit\n100\t   * logging for SUPER_ADMIN must be awaited to guarantee persistence of\n101\t   * critical security events. NestJS guards fully support both sync\n102\t   * (`boolean`) and async (`Promise<boolean>`) return types.\n103\t   */\n104\t  async canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {\n105\t    // Skip if endpoint is marked public\n106\t    const isPublic = this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<boolean>(\n107\t      IS_PUBLIC_KEY,\n108\t      [context.getHandler(), context.getClass()],\n109\t this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<boolean>(\n117\t      SKIP_TENANT_GUARD_KEY,\n118\t      [context.getHandler(), context.getClass()],\n119\t    );\n120\t\n121\t    if (skipGuard) {\n122\t      return GqlExecutionContext.create(context);\n130\t      request = gqlCtx.getContext().req as TenantRequest;\n131\t    } else {\n132\t      request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest<TenantRequest>();\n133\t
